float(np.quantile(vt, 0.975)))}\n\n\ndef sham_obs(res: dict[str, Any], boot: dict[str, list]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    """7.1: row floyd (t2): is s(bf_spfa) the largest residual and > 0; row iddfs (t1): same for dfs."""\n    out = {}\n    for t, B, j in (("t2", "floyd", "bf_spfa"), ("t1", "iddfs", "dfs")):\n        row = res["tasks"][t]["rows"].get(B)\n        if not row or j not in row["s"]:\n            out[f"{t}|{B}->{j}"] = None\n            continue\n        s = row["s"]\n        vals = [c["srow"][B][c["surv"][B].index(j)] for c in boot[t] if B in c["srow"]]\n        largest = [float(np.argmax(c["srow"][B]) == c["surv"][B].index(j)) for c in boot[t] if B in c["srow"]]\n        out[f"{t}|{B}->{j}"] = {"s_j": s[j], "is_largest_residual": max(s, key=s.get) == j,\n                                "residual_positive": s[j] > 0,\n                                "s_j_ci": (float(np.quantile(vals, 0.025)), float(np.quantile(vals, 0.975))),\n                                "boot_prob_largest": float(np.mean(largest)),\n                                "G_j": row["Gbar"][j], "fitted_ladder_j": row["fitted_ladder"][j]}\n    return out\n\n\ndef shams_and_two_step(tds: dict[str, TaskData]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    """7.3 off-catalogue shams (descriptive stratum) and 7.2 two-step landing under the frozen templates."""\n    out: dict[str, Any] = {"shams": {}, "two_step": {}}\n    for t in TASKS:\n        td = tds[t]\n        conds, virtual = needed_conds(t)\n        ca = CellArrays(td, conds, "primary", virtual)\n        cx = Ctx(ca, ca.cells())\n        js = ca.js\n        u = ladder_u(family_profiles(cx, WS, RAND_NAMES))\n        uc = u - u.mean()\n        uhat = uc / (np.linalg.norm(uc) + 1e-12)\n        for x in SHAMS[t]:\n            reps = []\n            for w in WS:\n                for h, ns in (("h1", "A"), ("h2", "B")):\n                    g = cx.v(f"ban|{x}|{w}", h) - cx.v(f"neutral|{x}|{w}|{ns}", h)\n                    reps.append(g - np.nanmean(g))\n            G = np.nanmean(reps, axis=0)\n            if not np.isfinite(G).all():\n                out["shams"][f"{t}|{x}"] = {"scored": False}\n                continue\n            a = float(G @ uhat)\n            s = G - a * uhat\n            order = [js[i] for i in np.argsort(-s)]\n            neigh = [j for j in SHAM_NEIGHBOURS[x] if j in js]\n            out["shams"][f"{t}|{x}"] = {"scored": True, "Gbar": dict(zip(js, G.tolist())),\n                                        "residual": dict(zip(js, s.tolist())), "a_ladder": a,\n                                        "residual_rank_order": order, "declared_neighbours": neigh,\n                                        "neighbours_top_k": bool(set(order[:len(neigh)]) == set(neigh)) if neigh else None,\n                                        "neighbour_mean_rank": float(np.mean([order.index(j) + 1 for j in neigh])) if neigh else None}\n        banned = {"t1": ["bfs", "dfs"], "t2": ["dijkstra", "bf_spfa"]}[t]\n        land = []\n        bm = []\n        for w in WS:\n            Lv = cx.v(f"ban|two_step|{w}")\n            if not np.isfinite(Lv).any():\n                continue\n            p = np.exp(Lv - np.nanmax(Lv))\n            p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)\n            p /= p.sum()\n            bm.append(float(sum(p[js.index(b)] for b in banned if b in js)))\n            allowed = [i for i, j in enumerate(js) if j not in banned]\n            q = p[allowed] / max(1e-300, p[allowed].sum())\n            land.append(dict(zip([js[i] for i in allowed], q.tolist())))\n        if land:\n            keys = land[0].keys()\n            out["two_step"][t] = {"implied_landing_frozen_templates": {k: float(np.mean([d[k] for d in land])) for k in keys},\n                                  "implied_banned_mass_frozen_templates": float(np.mean(bm)), "n_templates": len(land)}\n    return out\n\n\ndef _sens_summary(r: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    return {"pooled_share_point": r["pooled"]["share_point"], "pooled_bootstrap": r["pooled"].get("bootstrap"),\n            "pooled_branch": r["pooled"].get("branch"), "aim_prime_veto": r.get("aim_prime_veto"),\n            "per_task": {t: {"share_point": r["tasks"][t]["share_point"], "branch": r["tasks"][t].get("branch"),\n                             "aim": r["tasks"][t].get("aim")} for t in TASKS}}'}

```
{'notebook_path': '/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_2/art_6zBfl3kDjeiP/code_demo.ipynb', 'cell_id': 'cf39f4d3', 'edit_mode': 'insert', 'cell_type': 'code', 'new_source': 'RAND = ["A1001", "A1002", "A1003", "B2001", "B2002", "B2003"]\n\n\ndef lse(x):\n    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)\n    m = x.max()\n    return float(m + np.log(np.exp(x - m).sum()))\n\n\ndef load():\n    # notebook: per-program "total" scores from mini_demo_data.json (original: scores/cells/<task>/<uid>.parquet)\n    recs = []\n    for e in EXAMPLES:\n        for key, vals in e["scores"].items():\n            recs.append((e["task"], key, e["prog_id"], float(vals[META["score_columns"].index("total")])))\n    S = {}\n    for t, k, p, v in recs:\n        S.setdefault(t, {}).setdefault(k, {})[p] = v\n    return S\n\n\ndef main() -> dict[str, Any]:\n    S = load()\n    sel = EXAMPLES                                  # data/forced_selected.jsonl\n    yl = META["yields"]                             # results/yields.json -> "yields"\n    M = M_SCORED                                    # logs/throughput.json -> "M"\n    progs = {t: [r for r in sel if r["task"] == t and r["rank"] < min(r["m_j"], M)] for t in MODES}\n    meas = {t: [j for j in MODES[t] if yl[f"{t}|{j}"]["measurable"] and any(r["j"] == j for r in progs[t])]\n            for t in MODES}\n    lq = {}\n    for t in MODES:\n        for r in progs[t]:\n            v = [S[t].get(f"primary|forced|{r[\'j\']}|{r[\'family\']}|k{k}", {}).get(r["prog_id"]) for k in range(10)]\n            if all(x is not None for x in v):\n                lq[r["prog_id"]] = lse(v) - math.log(10)\n\n    def L(t, cond, j, half="all"):\n        key = cond if "|" in cond.split("|")[0] or cond.startswith(("primary|", "edit|", "edit2|")) else cond\n        key = ("edit" if cond.startswith("edit|") else "primary") + "|" + cond\n        ps = [r for r in progs[t] if r["j"] == j and (half == "all" or r["half"] == half)]\n        lw = [S[t][key][r["prog_id"]] - lq[r["prog_id"]] for r in ps if key in S[t] and r["prog_id"] in S[t][key]\n              and r["prog_id"] in lq]\n        if not lw:\n            return float("nan")\n        return math.log(yl[f"{t}|{j}"]["yield"]) + lse(lw) - math.log(len(lw))\n\n    def Lmix(t, condA, condB, j, half="all"):\n        """virtual neutral: noun A on h1, noun B on h2"""\n        ps = [r for r in progs[t] if r["j"] == j and (half == "all" or r["half"] == half)]\n        lw = []\n        for r in ps:\n            key = "primary|" + (condA if r["half"] == "h1" else condB)\n            if key in S[t] and r["prog_id"] in S[t][key] and r["prog_id"] in lq:\n                lw.append(S[t][key][r["prog_id"]] - lq[r["prog_id"]])\n        return math.log(yl[f"{t}|{j}"]["yield"]) + lse(lw) - math.log(len(lw)) if lw else float("nan")\n\n    out = {"checks": {}}\n    # 10 random FRLP cells\n    cells = frlp_cells                              # results/frlp_cells.jsonl\n    prim = [c for c in cells if c["variant"] == "primary" and not c["condition"].startswith("edit2")]\n    random.Random(3).shuffle(prim)\n    diffs = []\n    for c in prim[:10]:\n        v = L(c["task"], c["condition"], c["algorithm"], c["half"])\n        diffs.append(abs(v - c["L"]))\n    out["checks"]["frlp_cells_max_absdiff"] = max(diffs)\n    # gate (a)\n    gate = gate_out["primary"]                      # results/gate.json -> "primary"\n    ev = META["gate_events"]                        # results/gate_events.json\n    npara = GATE_PARAPHRASES                        # logs/throughput.json -> "gate_paraphrases"\n    Ss, ms = [], []\n    for t in MODES:\n        base = next(e for e in ev["events"] if e["event_id"] == f"{t}|baseline")\n        for p in base["paraphrases"][:npara]:\n            Ls = {j: L(t, f"exp1|baseline|p{p[\'paraphrase_id\']}", j) for j in meas[t]}\n            tot = sum(math.exp(v) for v in Ls.values())\n            Ss.append(tot)\n            ms.append(math.exp(Ls[{"t1": "bfs", "t2": "dijkstra"}[t]]) / tot)\n    out["checks"]["gate_a_S_absdiff"] = abs(np.mean(Ss) - gate["a"]["mean_S"])\n    out["checks"]["gate_a_m_absdiff"] = abs(np.mean(ms) - gate["a"]["mean_m_default"])\n    # gate (b)/(c)\n    des, hits, ncl = [], 0, 0\n    for e in ev["events"]:\n        t = e["task"]\n        allowed = [j for j in MODES[t] if j not in e["banned"]]\n        qs, ds = [], []\n        for p in e["paraphrases"][:npara]:\n            Ls = {j: L(t, f"exp1|{e[\'condition\']}|p{p[\'paraphrase_id\']}", j, "h1") for j in allowed if j in meas[t]}\n            mx = max(Ls.values())\n            z = sum(math.exp(v - mx) for v in Ls.values())\n            q = {j: (math.exp(Ls[j] - mx) / z if j in Ls else 0.0) for j in allowed}\n            qs.append(q)\n            vals = []\n            for lbl in p["labels"]:\n                lbl = lbl if lbl in MODES[t] else "other"\n                if lbl in e["banned"]:\n                    continue\n                vals.append(0.0 if lbl == "other" else math.log(0.93 * q[lbl] + 0.05 / len(allowed))\n                            - math.log(0.98 / len(allowed)))\n            ds.append(np.mean(vals) if vals else float("nan"))\n        des.append(np.nanmean(ds))\n        qbar = {j: np.mean([q[j] for q in qs]) for j in allowed}\n        if e["clear_mode"]:\n            ncl += 1\n            hits += int(max(qbar, key=qbar.get) == e["realised_mode"])\n    out["checks"]["gate_b_absdiff"] = abs(np.mean(des) - gate["b"]["mean_d_e"])\n    out["checks"]["gate_c_hits_equal"] = float(hits == gate["c"]["hits"] and ncl == gate["c"]["n_clear"])\n    # C1 point SHARE (primary reference) and AIM\n    c1 = c1_out["primary_neutral"]                  # results/c1.json -> "primary_neutral"\n    assoc = META["association_profiles"]            # results/association.json -> "profiles"\n    pooled = {"Vu": 0.0, "Vs": 0.0}\n    aims = []\n    placebo_rows = []\n    for t in MODES:\n        js = meas[t]\n        nonce = np.mean([[L(t, f"ban|nonce|{w}", j) - Lmix(t, f"neutral|nonce|{w}|A", f"neutral|nonce|{w}|B", j)\n                          for j in js] for w in WS], axis=0)\n        base = np.array([L(t, "base0", j) for j in js])\n        rand = np.mean([[L(t, f"edit|{d}", j) for j in js] for d in RAND], axis=0) - base\n        nov = np.array([L(t, "novelty", j) for j in js]) - base\n        u = (nonce + rand + nov) / 3\n        Vu = Vs = 0.0\n        for B in MODES[t]:\n            Sv = [j for j in js if j != B]\n            if len(Sv) < 3:\n                continue\n            idx = [js.index(j) for j in Sv]\n            reps, r_h = [], []\n            for w in WS:\n                for h, ns in (("h1", "A"), ("h2", "B")):\n                    g = np.array([L(t, f"ban|{B}|{w}", j, h) - L(t, f"neutral|{B}|{w}|{ns}", j, h) for j in Sv])\n                    reps.append(g - g.mean())\n                    r_h.append(h)\n            C = np.array(reps)\n            G = C.mean(0)\n            ub = u[idx] - u[idx].mean()\n            uh = ub / np.linalg.norm(ub)\n            a = G @ uh\n            s = G - a * uh\n            tot = C.var(axis=0, ddof=1).sum() / len(C)\n            sig = tot / (len(Sv) - 1)\n            Vu += a * a - sig\n            Vs += s @ s - (len(Sv) - 2) * sig\n            res_h = {}\n            for hn in ("h1", "h2"):\n                Gh = np.array([c for c, hh in zip(C, r_h) if hh == hn]).mean(0)\n                res_h[hn] = Gh - (Gh @ uh) * uh\n            pv = {}\n            for hn, ns in (("h1", "A"), ("h2", "B")):\n                pv[hn] = np.mean([[L(t, f"mention|{B}|{w}", j, hn) - L(t, f"neutral|{B}|{w}|{ns}", j, hn) for j in Sv]\n                                  for w in WS], axis=0)\n            rho = np.nanmean([spearmanr(res_h["h1"], pv["h2"]).correlation, spearmanr(res_h["h2"], pv["h1"]).correlation])\n            aims.append(rho)\n            placebo_rows.append((res_h, pv))\n        share = Vs / (Vs + Vu)\n        out["checks"][f"share_{t}_absdiff"] = abs(share - c1["tasks"][t]["share_point"]["SHARE"])\n        pooled["Vu"] += Vu\n        pooled["Vs"] += Vs\n    out["checks"]["share_pooled_absdiff"] = abs(pooled["Vs"] / (pooled["Vs"] + pooled["Vu"])\n                                                - c1["pooled"]["share_point"]["SHARE"])\n    aim_c1 = c1_out["primary_neutral"].get("aim_prime_veto", {})\n    out["checks"]["aim_absdiff"] = abs(float(np.nanmean(aims)) - aim_c1.get("aim", float("nan")))\n    # placebo: PV profiles shuffled across rows (template/row labels permuted) -> aim near its null\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(5)\n    pl = []\n    for _ in range(N_PLACEBO):\n        vals = []\n        for res_h, pv in placebo_rows:\n            perm = rng.permutation(len(pv["h1"]))\n            vals.append(np.nanmean([spearmanr(res_h["h1"], pv["h2"][perm]).correlation,\n                                    spearmanr(res_h["h2"], pv["h1"][perm]).correlation]))\n        pl.append(np.nanmean(vals))\n    out["placebo_aim"] = {"mean": float(np.mean(pl)), "sd": float(np.std(pl)), "observed": float(np.nanmean(aims))}\n    tol = 1e-9\n    out["all_match"] = bool(all((v < tol) if "absdiff" in k else (v == 1.0) for k, v in out["checks"].items()))\n    print(json.dumps(out, indent=1, default=float))   # the original also wrote results/independent_rederivation.json\n    return out\n\n\nt0 = time.time()\nrederivation = main()\nprint(f"re-derivation took {time.time() - t0:.1f}s")'}
```

```
import json, secrets
p = "code_demo.ipynb"
nb = json.load(open(p))
def lines(s): return s.splitlines(keepends=True)
c = next(c for c in nb["cells"] if c["id"] == "507e14fa")
c["source"] = lines("""Next comes the body of `analysis_misc.main()`. It builds the per-task score matrices, checks the mixture denominator against an independent numpy recomputation (validation v), builds every FRLP cell, and summarises the ESS per condition family (`ess_table()`).

The table at the end shows the instrument at work on T1:

* **Baseline.** Under the task alone (`base0`), FRLP gives DFS about as much mass as BFS, although iteration 1 sampled BFS almost every time. This is the calibration problem the gate detects in section 4.
* **Ban vs mention.** The BFS ban drops BFS by about 10 nats and lifts DFS. Its mention twin (*"You could use BFS"*) keeps BFS and pushes DFS down by more than 30 nats.
* **Generic perturbations** move mass too. The novelty instruction lifts A*, IDDFS and bidirectional BFS, and a random hidden-state edit (`edit|A1001`) lowers BFS.
* **Estimator noise.** Values above 0 mean an estimated probability above 1. Together with an ESS near 1 in almost every cell, this shows that single programs dominate the importance weights, so the analysis works with differences between conditions.""")
summary = """## Summary

With the default configuration (9 programs per algorithm, full-run bootstrap and null sizes), the demo reaches the full run's conclusions on less than half of the data:

* **FRLP validity gate: FAIL on calibration (a), pass on (b) and (c), as in the full run.** Under the T1 baseline, the name-scrubbed programs give DFS a large share (m = 0.76 < 0.9), although iteration 1 sampled BFS almost always. The **unscrubbed** programs pass all three criteria (m = 1.00). So the failure comes from scrubbing: a scrubbed program is much less likely under its own forced prompts (which name the algorithm) than under a neutral prompt, which inflates the weights of minority algorithms.
* **C1 share test (neutral twins): UNDETERMINED.** The pooled X-specific SHARE is 0.52 [0.21, 0.65] (full run 0.55 [0.25, 0.67]). The gains are far above replicate noise, so this is not pure subtraction, but the CI straddles 0.5.
* **No aimed substitute and no coherent ladder.** The X-specific residuals are not aimed at the prime-and-veto or association profiles (AIM ≈ 0, p ≈ 0.5). The 8 generic profiles disagree with each other (mean centred cosine 0.11 on T1, −0.07 on T2).
* **Bans prime the banned name.** In 8 of 11 rows, banning an algorithm raises its own FRLP. Examples: +26.5 nats for bidirectional BFS, +22.2 for IDDFS, +21.0 for Dial. Only the bans of the modal algorithms (BFS, Dijkstra on T2) and of A* lower it, the same pattern as in the full run.
* **Where the subset differs from the full run.**
  * With fewer programs per cell, the mention-twin and ESS-weighted references turn X-SPECIFIC-UNAIMED, so the pooled branch is not robust here (it was in the full run).
  * The `correct_only` variant becomes unstable.
  * Nearly every cell rests on an ESS close to 1, so all numbers are descriptive, as in the full run.
* **Audit.** The independent re-derivation matches every headline number (differences ≤ 1e-15), and the placebo AIM sits at its null.

**Checking against the full run.** `build_mini_demo_data.py --m 20` builds the full score data (211 programs). With `M_SCORED = 20`, the code in this notebook reproduces the artifact's published gate, C1 SHARE, AIM and sensitivity numbers to within 1e-7; the scores are stored with 6 decimals."""
nb["cells"].append({"cell_type": "markdown", "id": secrets.token_hex(4), "metadata": {}, "source": lines(summary)})
json.dump(nb, open(p, "w"), indent=1, ensure_ascii=False)
print(len(nb["cells"]), "cells")
EOF
ls -la
```

````
# Demo: do algorithm bans pick a substitute or a ladder? (FRLP screen on Qwen3-8B)

This folder turns the experiment artifact *"Do algorithm bans pick a substitute or a ladder?"* (round 2,
experiment 7) into a notebook that runs top to bottom in about two minutes on a 2-CPU machine (Google Colab or
local Jupyter).

**The question.** When a prompt bans an algorithm ("Do not use BFS for this task"), the probability mass that
leaves it can go to two kinds of place:

* a **specific substitute** chosen for that ban;
* a **generic ladder**, i.e. the same profile that any perturbation of the prompt produces.

**The instrument.** Forced-ratio latent probabilities (FRLP): importance-sampled estimates of
log P(model writes algorithm j | prompt), computed from teacher-forced log-likelihoods of forced, name-scrubbed
programs. The model is Qwen3-8B and there are two path-search tasks:

* T1, grid path, with 6 algorithms;
* T2, weighted shortest path, with 5 algorithms.

The notebook keeps the artifact's own code, split into cells with an explanation before each section:

* `method.py`, the stage orchestrator;
* `src/config.py`, the frozen protocol;
* `src/frlp.py` and `src/analysis_misc.py`, the FRLP cells;
* `src/gate.py`, the validity gate;
* `src/c1.py`, the C1 share test, AIM, sensitivity, shams and two-step bans;
* `audit/rederive.py`, the independent re-derivation;
* `src/outputs.py`, the figures F1–F6.

## Layout

| path | what it is |
|---|---|
| `code_demo.ipynb` | The demo notebook, saved with the outputs of a complete run in a clean Python 3.12 environment. |
| `mini_demo_data.json` | Demo data (1.5 MB): 99 of the 211 scored forced programs (the first 9 per algorithm by their pre-registered selection rank), each with raw and name-scrubbed code and its log-likelihoods `[total, body, cum256]` under every scored condition (17,625 scores). It also holds the side tables the analysis reads (yields, the 19 gate events with their sampled labels, free-form association profiles, prompt segments and nouns) and the full run's headline numbers. |
| `build_mini_demo_data.py` | Rebuilds `mini_demo_data.json` from the artifact folder (`--m` sets the programs kept per algorithm). |
| `logs/build_mini_demo_data.log` | Log of the data builds. |
| `logs/nbtest_round{0,1,2}.log` | `nbconvert` test runs in a fresh venv: minimum, 10% and full-run statistical settings. |
| `.aii/manifest.yaml` | Storage manifest. It has no entries, because nothing in this folder is heavy. |

## How to run

* **Colab:** open `code_demo.ipynb` and run all cells. The first cell installs `loguru` and `rapidfuzz`, and the data is fetched from this repository's raw GitHub URL.
* **Local (Python 3.12):**

  ```bash
  pip install jupyter ipykernel
  jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb
  ```

  Outside Colab, the install cell also pins numpy 2.0.2, pandas 2.2.2, scipy 1.16.3 and matplotlib 3.10.0, which are Colab's versions. If the GitHub URL is unreachable, the notebook falls back to the local `mini_demo_data.json`.
* **Rebuild the data** from the artifact folder (the folder holding `method.py`, `scores/`, `data/`, `results/`, `prompts/` and `logs/`; in the published repository it is the parent of this folder):

  ```bash
  uv run --no-project --python 3.12 --with pandas==2.2.2 --with pyarrow==18.1.0 --with loguru==0.7.3 \
      python build_mini_demo_data.py --src ..
  ```

## Configuration and measured runtime

All tunable parameters sit in one configuration cell. The bootstrap, permutation and placebo sizes are at the
full run's values. `M_SCORED` is the one parameter limited by the demo data: it is 9 here, against 20 in the full
run. Test runs (`nbconvert`, fresh venv, 2 CPUs, kernel time only):

| round | M_SCORED | gate paraphrases | N_BOOT / N_PERM | SENS_BOOT / SENS_PERM | N_PLACEBO | runtime |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 (minimum) | 7 | 1 | 10 / 1,000 | 5 / 500 | 5 | 35 s |
| 1 | 9 | 3 | 200 / 20,000 | 40 / 2,000 | 20 | 39 s |
| 2 (final = full-run statistics) | 9 | 3 | 2,000 / 200,000 | 400 / 20,000 | 200 | 74 s |

## Results of the demo (default configuration) vs the full run

| quantity | demo (99 programs, M = 9) | full run (211 programs, M = 20) |
|---|---|---|
| FRLP gate | FAIL on (a): S = 0.50, m = 0.76; (b) 1.18 nats; (c) 17/19 | FAIL on (a): S = 1.02, m = 0.83; (b) 0.92 nats; (c) 16/19 |
| gate, unscrubbed programs | passes (a), (b) and (c) | passes (a), (b) and (c) |
| C1 pooled SHARE, neutral twins | 0.52 [0.21, 0.65], UNDETERMINED | 0.55 [0.25, 0.67], UNDETERMINED |
| C1 pooled SHARE, mention twins | 0.77 [0.61, 0.85], X-SPECIFIC-UNAIMED | 0.66 [0.49, 0.87], UNDETERMINED |
| pooled branch robust across references | no | yes |
| AIM vs prime-and-veto profile | -0.00 (p = 0.51) | -0.10 (p = 0.80) |
| generic profiles agree (ladder exists) | no (cos 0.11 T1, -0.07 T2) | no (cos 0.34 T1, -0.02 T2) |
| two-step ban landing | T1: IDDFS 0.73, A* 0.27; T2: Floyd-Warshall 1.00 | same |
| independent re-derivation | all checks match (<= 1e-15) | all checks match |

The demo reaches the full run's main conclusions:

* the gate fails, and it fails because of name scrubbing (the unscrubbed programs pass);
* C1 is UNDETERMINED under the primary reference;
* there is no aimed substitute and no coherent generic ladder;
* bans of rarely-written algorithms prime the banned name.

With fewer programs per cell, the mention-twin and ESS-weighted references move to X-SPECIFIC-UNAIMED. As in the
full run, nearly every FRLP cell has an effective sample size near 1, so every number is descriptive.

## Faithfulness of the conversion

To check the conversion, the data was built for all 211 programs (`--m 20`, a temporary file that is not kept),
and the notebook's code was run on it with `M_SCORED = 20` and `GATE_PARAPHRASES = 3`. It reproduces the artifact's
published results:

* the gate statistics and every variant's pass/fail to within 1e-7;
* all C1 SHARE point estimates (three references, both tasks, pooled) and all 12 sensitivity SHAREs to within 1e-7;
* the AIM values, null 95th percentiles and p-values exactly;
* the two-step landings.

The remaining differences of about 1e-7 come from storing the scores with 6 decimals.

## Changes relative to the artifact code

* File reads go to the loaded data instead of the artifact's files:
  * `scores/cells/*.parquet` and `scores/units_done.jsonl` (`load_scores`, `rederive.load`);
  * `data/forced_selected.jsonl`, `results/yields.json`, `results/gate_events.json` and `results/association.json`;
  * the `M` and `gate_paraphrases` values from `logs/throughput.json`, which become config variables.
* Result files become notebook variables (`frlp_cells`, `gate_out`, `c1_out`, `sens`, `secondary`, `rederivation`), and figures are shown inline instead of being saved.
* `method.py`'s `run()` (one subprocess per stage) is replaced by a printout of the stage table. The GPU stages (vLLM generation, teacher-forced scoring) and the stages that need the iteration-1 suite are not re-run; their outputs are what the data file holds.
* Left out:
  * `frlp.coverage()` and `analysis_misc.bookkeeping()`, which need iteration-1 programs and run logs;
  * `freeze.py`, which writes the hash-committed files for the confirmation bench.
* Logging goes to stdout only, and the expected NumPy "Mean of empty slice" warnings (from conditions that were never scored) are silenced.

## Restoring removed files

Nothing in this folder is marked for deletion: `.aii/manifest.yaml` has no entries, so nothing needs restoring.

* The notebook's Python packages install from PyPI in its first cell.
* `mini_demo_data.json` can be rebuilt from the artifact folder with the `build_mini_demo_data.py` command above.
* The full teacher-forced scores (`scores/`, about 3.3 GPU-hours) stay with the artifact itself, not in this folder.
````

(FRLP screen on Qwen3-8B)\n\nThis folder turns the experiment artifact *\"Do algorithm bans pick a substitute or a ladder?\"* (round 2,\nexperiment 7) into a notebook that runs top to bottom in about two minutes on a 2-CPU machine (Google Colab or\nlocal Jupyter).\n\n**The question.** When a prompt bans an algorithm (\"Do not use BFS for this task\"), the probability mass that\nleaves it can go to two kinds of place:\n\n* a **specific substitute** chosen for that ban;\n* a **generic ladder**, i.e. the same profile that any perturbation of the prompt produces.\n\n**The instrument.** Forced-ratio latent probabilities (FRLP): importance-sampled estimates of\nlog P(model writes algorithm j | prompt), computed from teacher-forced log-likelihoods of forced, name-scrubbed\nprograms. The model is Qwen3-8B and there are two path-search tasks:\n\n* T1, grid path, with 6 algorithms;\n* T2, weighted shortest path, with 5 algorithms.\n\nThe notebook keeps the artifact's own code, split into cells with an explanation before each section:\n\n* `method.py`, the stage orchestrator;\n* `src/config.py`, the frozen protocol;\n* `src/frlp.py` and `src/analysis_misc.py`, the FRLP cells;\n* `src/gate.py`, the validity gate;\n* `src/c1.py`, the C1 share test, AIM, sensitivity, shams and two-step bans;\n* `audit/rederive.py`, the independent re-derivation;\n* `src/outputs.py`, the figures F1–F6.\n\n## Layout\n\n| path | what it is |\n|---|---|\n| `code_demo.ipynb` | The demo notebook, saved with the outputs of a complete run in a clean Python 3.12 environment. |\n| `mini_demo_data.json` | Demo data (1.5 MB): 99 of the 211 scored forced programs (the first 9 per algorithm by their pre-registered selection rank), each with raw and name-scrubbed code and its log-likelihoods `[total, body, cum256]` under every scored condition (17,625 scores). It also holds the side tables the analysis reads (yields, the 19 gate events with their sampled labels, free-form association profiles, prompt segments and nouns) and the full run's headline numbers. |\n| `build_mini_demo_data.py` | Rebuilds `mini_demo_data.json` from the artifact folder (`--m` sets the programs kept per algorithm). |\n| `logs/build_mini_demo_data.log` | Log of the data builds. |\n| `logs/nbtest_round{0,1,2}.log` | `nbconvert` test runs in a fresh venv: minimum, 10% and full-run statistical settings. |\n| `.aii/manifest.yaml` | Storage manifest. It has no entries, because nothing in this folder is heavy. |\n\n## How to run\n\n* **Colab:** open `code_demo.ipynb` and run all cells. The first cell installs `loguru` and `rapidfuzz`, and the data is fetched from this repository's raw GitHub URL.\n* **Local (Python 3.12):**\n\n  ```bash\n  pip install jupyter ipykernel\n  jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb\n  ```\n\n  Outside Colab, the install cell also pins numpy 2.0.2, pandas 2.2.2, scipy 1.16.3 and matplotlib 3.10.0, which are Colab's versions. If the GitHub URL is unreachable, the notebook falls back to the local `mini_demo_data.json`.\n* **Rebuild the data** from the artifact folder (the folder holding `method.py`, `scores/`, `data/`, `results/`, `prompts/` and `logs/`; in the published repository it is the parent of this folder):\n\n  ```bash\n  uv run --no-project --python 3.12 --with pandas==2.2.2 --with pyarrow==18.1.0 --with loguru==0.7.3 \\\n      python build_mini_demo_data.py --src ..\n  ```\n\n## Configuration and measured runtime\n\nAll tunable parameters sit in one configuration cell. The bootstrap, permutation and placebo sizes are at the\nfull run's values. `M_SCORED` is the one parameter limited by the demo data: it is 9 here, against 20 in the full\nrun. Test runs (wall time of `jupyter nbconvert --execute` in a fresh venv on 2 CPUs, including the install cell):\n\n| round | M_SCORED | gate paraphrases | N_BOOT / N_PERM | SENS_BOOT / SENS_PERM | N_PLACEBO | runtime |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 0 (minimum) | 7 | 1 | 10 / 1,000 | 5 / 500 | 5 | 35 s |\n| 1 | 9 | 3 | 200 / 20,000 | 40 / 2,000 | 20 | 39 s |\n| 2 (final = full-run statistics) | 9 | 3 | 2,000 / 200,000 | 400 / 20,000 | 200 | 74 s |\n\n## Results of the demo (default configuration) vs the full run\n\n| quantity | demo (99 programs, M = 9) | full run (211 programs, M = 20) |\n|---|---|---|\n| FRLP gate | FAIL on (a): S = 0.50, m = 0.76; (b) 1.18 nats; (c) 17/19 | FAIL on (a): S = 1.02, m = 0.83; (b) 0.92 nats; (c) 16/19 |\n| gate, unscrubbed programs | passes (a), (b) and (c) | passes (a), (b) and (c) |\n| C1 pooled SHARE, neutral twins | 0.52 [0.21, 0.65], UNDETERMINED | 0.55 [0.25, 0.67], UNDETERMINED |\n| C1 pooled SHARE, mention twins | 0.77 [0.61, 0.85], X-SPECIFIC-UNAIMED | 0.66 [0.49, 0.87], UNDETERMINED |\n| pooled branch robust across references | no | yes |\n| AIM vs prime-and-veto profile | -0.00 (p = 0.51) | -0.10 (p = 0.80) |\n| generic profiles agree (ladder exists) | no (cos 0.11 T1, -0.07 T2) | no (cos 0.34 T1, -0.02 T2) |\n| two-step ban landing | T1: IDDFS 0.73, A* 0.27; T2: Floyd-Warshall 1.00 | same |\n| independent re-derivation | all checks match (<= 1e-15) | all checks match |\n\nThe demo reaches the full run's main conclusions:\n\n* the gate fails, and it fails because of name scrubbing (the unscrubbed programs pass);\n* C1 is UNDETERMINED under the primary reference;\n* there is no aimed substitute and no coherent generic ladder;\n* bans of rarely-written algorithms prime the banned name.\n\nWith fewer programs per cell, the mention-twin and ESS-weighted references move to X-SPECIFIC-UNAIMED. As in the\nfull run, nearly every FRLP cell has an effective sample size near 1, so every number is descriptive.\n\n## Faithfulness of the conversion\n\nTo check the conversion, the data was built for all 211 programs (`--m 20`, a temporary file that is not kept),\nand the notebook's code was run on it with `M_SCORED = 20` and `GATE_PARAPHRASES = 3`. It reproduces the artifact's\npublished results:\n\n* the gate statistics and every variant's pass/fail to within 1e-7;\n* all C1 SHARE point estimates (three references, both tasks, pooled) and all 12 sensitivity SHAREs to within 1e-7;\n* the AIM values, null 95th percentiles and p-values exactly;\n* the two-step landings.\n\nThe remaining differences of about 1e-7 come from storing the scores with 6 decimals.\n\n## Changes relative to the artifact code\n\n* File reads go to the loaded data instead of the artifact's files:\n  * `scores/cells/*.parquet` and `scores/units_done.jsonl` (`load_scores`, `rederive.load`);\n  * `data/forced_selected.jsonl`, `results/yields.json`, `results/gate_events.json` and `results/association.json`;\n  * the `M` and `gate_paraphrases` values from `logs/throughput.json`, which become config variables.\n* Result files become notebook variables (`frlp_cells`, `gate_out`, `c1_out`, `sens`, `secondary`, `rederivation`), and figures are shown inline instead of being saved.\n* `method.py`'s `run()` (one subprocess per stage) is replaced by a printout of the stage table. The GPU stages (vLLM generation, teacher-forced scoring) and the stages that need the iteration-1 suite are not re-run; their outputs are what the data file holds.\n* Left out:\n  * `frlp.coverage()` and `analysis_misc.bookkeeping()`, which need iteration-1 programs and run logs;\n  * `freeze.py`, which writes the hash-committed files for the confirmation bench.\n* Logging goes to stdout only, and the expected NumPy \"Mean of empty slice\" warnings (from conditions that were never scored) are silenced.\n\n## Restoring removed files\n\nNothing in this folder is marked for deletion: `.aii/manifest.yaml` has no entries, so nothing needs restoring.\n\n* The notebook's Python packages install from PyPI in its first cell.\n* `mini_demo_data.json` can be rebuilt from the artifact folder with the `build_mini_demo_data.py` command above.\n* The full teacher-forced scores (`scores/`, about 3.3 GPU-hours) stay with the artifact itself, not in this folder.\n", "structuredPatch": [{"oldStart": 35, "oldLines": 7, "newStart": 35, "newLines": 7, "lines": [" | `mini_demo_data.json` | Demo data (1.5 MB): 99 of the 211 scored forced programs (the first 9 per algorithm by their pre-registered selection rank), each with raw and name-scrubbed code and its log-likelihoods `[total, body, cum256]` under every scored condition (17,625 scores). **What the notebook runs.** `method.py` itself only launches stage scripts, so the cells hold the code those stages actually run (the FRLP cells, the gate, the C1 share test, the audit and the figures). The original code is kept, with only file reads and writes swapped for the loaded data. The GPU stages (generating programs with vLLM and scoring them with Qwen3-8B, about 3.3 GPU-hours) can't run in a demo. Their output is what `mini_demo_data.json` holds: 99 of the 211 scored programs (the first 9 per algorithm), each with its scores under every prompt condition, 1.5 MB in total.

```
**Settings.** I scaled up in three rounds (35 s, 39 s, 74 s). The bootstrap, permutation and placebo sizes are now at the full run's values. The one setting the data limits is programs per algorithm: 9 here instead of 20, because of the 100-example cap.

**Results against the full run:**
- **Validity gate:** fails on calibration (a) and passes (b) and (c), as in the full run. The unscrubbed programs pass all three, which is the full run's diagnosis.
- **Share test:** the pooled share is 0.52 [0.21, 0.65] (full run 0.55 [0.25, 0.67]), so the verdict stays UNDETERMINED.
- **Same as the full run:** no aimed substitute, no coherent ladder, the same priming pattern, and identical two-step landings.
- **Difference:** with fewer programs, the mention-twin reference gives a different verdict, so the verdict is not stable across references here as it was in the full run.

**Check that the conversion is faithful.** I built a temporary file with all 211 programs and ran the notebook's code on it (then deleted the file). It reproduces the artifact's published numbers: the gate, all share-test values and the sensitivity variants agree within 1e-7, and the aim-test values and p-values are identical. The small differences come from storing scores with 6 decimals.

**Deviations:**
- There was no todo-tracking tool in this session, so I followed the six TODOs in order.
- I created the notebook with NotebookEdit. Once it grew too large for the Read tool (which NotebookEdit needs first), I made the later edits by editing the notebook JSON with small Python scripts.
- The end of the pasted text (the LLM-creativity brief) looked like the wider research context, so I didn't start that research here.

`.aii/manifest.yaml` has no entries because nothing in the folder is heavy, and `README.md` covers the layout, run commands, runtimes, results and a "Restoring removed files" section. The structured result is in `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`.

Files are in the workspace folder:
- `code_demo.ipynb`
- `mini_demo_data.json`
- `build_mini_demo_data.py` (rebuilds the data file from the artifact folder)
```
